UNVERIFIED; no actual C78 seed or new gate.", "evidence": "# Full primitive certificates: cut slack and quantitative prefix control\n\nStatus: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4's final\nauthorized round23. Fact04219554e41d9750 supplies the precise full seed\ninterface and its conditional asymptotic consequence, but does not verify\nthe new statements below. No seed existence or nonexistence is claimed.\nNo optimizer, numerical evaluation, finite replay, or fact_submit is used.\n\nNovelty relative to the shared record: f7cee3034df8cb7f already derived the\nexact saturated-cut structure and the signed tail credit. The new content\nhere is an exact positive-slack budget, including steps below1, its full\nmatrix consequences, and explicit quantitative control of a prefix when\na cut is near equality. The equality case is rederived as a consistency\ncheck; the old claim of automatic nonnegative tail restriction is not used.\nAll new implications are proved together here; no unverified finding is a\npremise. The accepted inputs are04219554e41d9750 and1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 1. Full formal interface and the gradient-difference matrix\n\nLet r>=1, h_0,...,h_(r-1)>=0, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t for0<=i<=r,\ns=P_r, and C=1+s. Set h_r=0 only for formulas. Indices of the formal\ngradients range over0,...,r. Let e be the all-ones column, and define\n\n    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n    D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)),\n    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,  M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.\n\nAssume Lambda_ii=0, all off-diagonal Lambda_ij>=0, L^T e=omega,\nand M>=0 on the entire original space R^(r+1). Thus this is precisely\nthe full finite certificate in fact04219554e41d9750, with arbitrary r.\nNo multiplier support, rank, or equality between formal gradients is imposed.\n\nFor x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, define\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)+sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                               -s(s+1)||g_r||^2.\n\nThe full coefficient identity from fact04219554e41d9750 is\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = <M,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.       (1)\n\nThe residuals Q_ij are nonpositive on every actual convex differentiable\n1-smooth trajectory by fact1252b8823bb5a290. Hence this interface implies\nV_h>=0 on all such trajectories with a minimizer.\n\nHere is an algebraic proof of the constant-gradient kernel, needed below.\nSince Te=e+(P_0,...,P_r)^T and\n2sum_(i<r)h_i P_i=s^2-sum_(i<r)h_i^2, we have\n\n    e^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2,\n    e^T LT e=omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2.\n\nThus e^T M e=0. Positive semidefiniteness implies Me=0: otherwise for\nsome y the linear term in (e+t y)^T M(e+t y) would be negative for one\nsign of sufficiently small t.\n\nFor0<=j<r let u^j be the indicator of indices0,...,j. Let U have these\nr columns and put K_jump=U^T M U. For any scalar formal vector g,\n\n    g=e*g_r+sum_(j<r)u^j(g_j-g_(j+1)),\n    g^T M g=sum_(i,j<r)(K_jump)_ij(g_i-g_(i+1))(g_j-g_(j+1)). (2)\n\nIn particular K_jump>=0. Conversely, under Me=0, its PSD property is\nequivalent to full M>=0 by(2). This is a congruence identity retaining\nall formal gradients, not a deletion of zero-step coordinates.\n\n## 2. A legal cut trajectory for every positive step\n\nFix j with b=h_j>0. In this section define\n\n    P=P_j, R=s-P_(j+1), A=1+P, B=1+R, d=b-1,\n    v=1-b                 if 0<b<=1,\n    v=(1-b)/B             if b>=1,\n    w=1-v>0.\n\nThe two formulas agree at b=1. There is one legal scalar convex1-smooth\nfunction with a minimizer whose GD trajectory has gradients1 at all\nindices i<=j and v at all indices k>j, starting from x_0=A. Its positions\nare\n\n    x_i=1+P_j-P_i                         (i<=j),\n    x_k=1-b-v(P_k-P_(j+1))                (k>j).             (3)\n\nAll sampled values can be chosen as f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.\n\nFor b>1, use the derivative clip(x,[v,1]) and f(0)=0. The bridge lands\nat -d=vB, the suffix remains at or below v, and its terminal point is v.\nThe derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and0 is a\nminimizer. On each plateau the stated values follow by integration.\n\nFor0<b<=1, v=1-b>=0. Put zeta=v(1-R)-1, strictly below every suffix\nsample and below v. Define a derivative on the whole real line by\n\n    f'(x)=0                  for x<=zeta-v,\n          x-zeta+v           for zeta-v<=x<=zeta,\n          v                  for zeta<=x<=v,\n          x                  for v<=x<=1,\n          1                  for x>=1.\n\nChoose the integration constant by f(v)=v^2/2. This derivative is again\ncontinuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and its zero region contains\nminimizers. The bridge lands at v. The suffix stays in[zeta,v], so its\ngradient is v. Integration gives the stated sampled values on both\nplateaus. Degenerate intervals when v=0 cause no difficulty. The minimum\nvalue need not be zero; V_h and every finite Q are invariant under adding\na constant to f.\n\nFor any scalar data satisfying f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, telescoping the GD\nsquares gives\n\n    V_h=x_0^2-(x_r+s g_r)^2.\n\nOn(3), x_r=-d-vR, hence the exact energy is\n\n    E_j=A^2-[(A+d)v-d]^2.                                (4)\n\nThe same sampled-value identity gives Q_ik=(g_i-g_k)(x_k-g_k).\nDefine, for k>j,\n\n    t_jk=d+v(1+P_k-P_(j+1)).\n\nThese numbers are nonnegative, with the more transparent forms\n\n    t_jk=v(P_k-P_(j+1))          if0<b<=1,\n    t_jk=(-v)(s-P_k)             ifb>=1.\n\nEvery within-block residual is zero. Every cross residual is exactly\n\n    -Q_(i,k)=w*t_jk,\n    -Q_(k,i)=w*(P_j-P_i),              i<=j<k.             (5)\n\nSince g=v e+w u^j and Me=0, substitution into the complete identity(1)\ngives the exact slack budget\n\n    E_j=w^2(K_jump)_jj\n       +w sum_(i<=j<k)[t_jk Lambda_(i,k)\n                                      +(P_j-P_i)Lambda_(k,i)]. (6)\n\nEvery term on the right is nonnegative. Equation(6) keeps every original\ncross multiplier, including both directions and both endpoints.\n\n## 3. Step-only matrix bounds and a quadratic compatibility test\n\nFor each positive h_j define beta_j=E_j/w^2 using the preceding formulas.\nA feasible full certificate necessarily has beta_j>=0 and\n\n    0<=(K_jump)_jj<=beta_j.                              (7)\n\nWhen b>=1, the expression simplifies to\n\n    beta_j=[A^2 B^2-C^2 d^2]/(B+d)^2,\n    C=A+B+d.                                             (8)\n\nFor0<b<=1 it is\n\n    beta_j=[A^2-(1-b)^2(A+b)^2]/b^2.                     (9)\n\nNo negative-v trajectory is used in deriving(9). In particular the\nsmall-step case is not obtained by applying a wrong-sign Huber residual.\n\nFor this section suppose all steps are positive. Set beta_(-1)=beta_r=0\nand Drow_i=sqrt(beta_(i-1))+sqrt(beta_i) for0<=i<=r.\nFor a PSD matrix, its2-by-2 principal minors give\n|(K_jump)_ik|<=sqrt(beta_i beta_k). Equation(2), applied to a standard\ncoordinate vector, therefore gives\n\n    M_ii<=Drow_i^2,\n    |(M u^j)_i|<=Drow_i sqrt(beta_j).                    (10)\n\nThe second inequality is the PSD Cauchy--Schwarz inequality; it also\nfollows by requiring (e_i+t u^j)^T M(e_i+t u^j)>=0 for every real t.\nFor every real vector z of length r,\n\n    0<=z^T K_jump z<=(sum_(i<r)|z_i|sqrt(beta_i))^2.      (11)\n\nAn additional necessary condition involves no unknown multipliers.\nPut p_0=1, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i. The actual quadratic f(x)=x^2/2,\nstarted at1, has x_i=g_i=p_i and every finite Q_ik=0. Consequently\n\n    V_h=1-C^2 p_r^2,\n    0<=1-C^2 p_r^2\n          <=(sum_(i<r)|h_i p_i|sqrt(beta_i))^2.           (12)\n\nThe upper inequality follows from(2),(11), since p_i-p_(i+1)=h_i p_i.\nEquation(12) is only a necessary compatibility test. Neither it nor the\nscalar inequalities beta_j>=0 are asserted sufficient.\n\n## 4. Explicit stability at a cut with b>1\n\nAssume all h_i>0 and fix a cut j with b=h_j>1. Continue using the local\nnotation P,R,A,B,d above, and set\n\n    a=d/B, w=1+a, delta=beta_j,\n    m=min_(i<r)h_i>0, Delta_cut=AB-Cd.\n\nEquation(8) implies Delta_cut>=0 and d<B: if d>=B, then Cd>=CB>AB.\nWrite u=Lambda_(j,r), v_k=Lambda_(k,j) for k>j, and set\nc_k=h_k for j<k<r, c_r=1; thus sum_(k>j)c_k=B.\n\nThe ideal cross array has only the entries\n\n    Lambda^0_(j,r)=d q, Lambda^0_(k,j)=q c_k (k>j),\n    q=2(A+d)/(B-d),                                     (13)\n\nwith every other cross entry zero. At exact cut equality this is the\nusual primitive-join array. Away from equality it is just an explicitly\ndefined comparison array; it is not claimed to be a certificate.\n\nHere are step-only error bounds, with empty sums interpreted as zero:\n\n    Fbd=w delta/(a m) if j<r-1, and Fbd=0 otherwise,\n    Bbd=w delta/m     if j>0,   and Bbd=0 otherwise,\n    Ubd=[2 sqrt(delta)sum_(i<j)Drow_i+P Bbd]/B,\n    eta=Fbd+Bbd+Ubd,\n    Ebd=2 sqrt(delta)sum_(k>j)Drow_k+2C(r-j)eta,\n    Lbd=[2B/(B-d)] [eta+Ebd/d].                         (14)\n\nThen the sum of the absolute differences of ALL crossing coefficients\nin the supplied full array and(13) is at most Lbd:\n\n    sum_(i<=j<k)(|Lambda_(i,k)-Lambda^0_(i,k)|\n                      +|Lambda_(k,i)-Lambda^0_(k,i)|)<=Lbd. (15)\n\nHere is a derivation, keeping track of the off-support terms.\nFirst (6) with t_jk=a(s-P_k) yields\n\n    Factual=sum_(i<=j,j<k<r)Lambda_(i,k)<=Fbd,\n    Bactual=sum_(k>j,i<j)Lambda_(k,i)<=Bbd.              (16)\n\nThe weights s-P_k and P_j-P_i in these sums are at least m. The\nremaining potentially unwanted cross terms are u_i=Lambda_(i,r),i<j.\nFor i<j, summing the complete mixed matrix entries gives\n\n    2(M 1_right)_i\n      =-B u_i-sum_(j<l<r)(1+P_l-P_(j+1))Lambda_(i,l)\n       -sum_(k>j)Lambda_(k,i)\n       +h_i sum_(k>j,l<=i)Lambda_(k,l).                 (17)\n\nIn(17), 1_right=e-u^j. By Me=0 and(10), the absolute value of its left\nside is at most2Drow_i sqrt(delta). The two subtracted sums are\nnonnegative. Thus\n\n    B u_i<=2Drow_i sqrt(delta)+h_i Bactual.\n\nSumming gives Uactual=sum_(i<j)u_i<=Ubd. Hence the total mass of all\ncross entries absent from(13) is at most eta.\n\nFor completeness, the full off-diagonal formula for i<k is\n\n    2M_ik=-(Lambda_ik+Lambda_ki)\n          -h_k sum_(l>k)Lambda_il+h_i sum_(l<=i)Lambda_kl. (18)\n\nIt follows directly by multiplying L and T; set h_r=0 in this formula.\nFor fixed k>j, summing absolute values in(18) over i<j gives\n\n    sum_(i<j)|2M_ik|\n       <=(1+h_k)(Factual+Uactual)+(1+P)Bactual<=C eta.\n\nSince (M u^j)_k=M_kj+sum_(i<j)M_ki, equation(10) gives\n|2M_jk|<=2Drow_k sqrt(delta)+C eta.\nFormula(18) at i=j gives, for k<r,\n\n    2M_jk=d v_k-h_k u\n        -Lambda_jk-h_k sum_(k<l<r)Lambda_jl\n                                  +b sum_(l<j)Lambda_kl,\n\nand at k=r gives 2M_jr=d v_r-u+b sum_(l<j)Lambda_rl.\nThe terms after d v_k-c_k u have total absolute value at most C eta.\nTherefore, for every k>j,\n\n    |d v_k-c_k u|<=e_k,\n    e_k=2Drow_k sqrt(delta)+2C eta,\n    sum_(k>j)e_k=Ebd.                                  (19)\n\nSum the original value-flow equations over the left block. Its outward\nflow is u+Uactual+Factual and its inward flow is sum v_k+Bactual. Thus\n\n    |sum_(k>j)v_k-u-2(A+d)|<=eta.\n\nTogether with(19) this implies\n\n    |u-d q|<=[d eta+Ebd]/(B-d)=:Uerr,\n    |v_k-q c_k|<=[c_k Uerr+e_k]/d.\n\nAdding these bounds and the off-support mass eta gives\n\n    eta+(1+B/d)Uerr+Ebd/d\n        =[2B/(B-d)] [eta+Ebd/d]=Lbd,\n\nwhich proves(15). In particular this controls the entire cross array,\nnot a selected sparse ansatz. No assertion about the internal arrays'\nsigns after tail restriction has entered the calculation.\n\n## 5. Quantitative primitive-energy bound for the actual prefix\n\nLet h_left=(h_0,...,h_(j-1)), of sum P and capacity A. Under the\nhypotheses of Section4, EVERY actual convex1-smooth trajectory of this\nprefix obeys\n\n    V_(h_left)>=-[Rbd+(2P+b+1/2)Lbd] Gmax^2,             (20)\n    Rbd=(A+d)Delta_cut/(B-d),\n    Gmax=max_(0<=i<=j)||g_i||.\n\nThe energy of an empty prefix is0. For a nonempty prefix, no initial\ndistance normalization or bounded minimizer distance is assumed in(20).\n\nTo prove the claim, take any actual prefix data. Translate positions\nand shift values so x_j=0 and f_j=0. Append formal tail data by setting\nevery g_k=f_k=0 for k>j and x_k=-b g_j for k>j. These tail data are NOT\nasserted to arise from an actual function. They are permitted for\nevaluating the full formal PSD identity(1). All tail-internal Q vanish.\n\nUsing the ideal cross array(13), direct substitution yields\n\n    V_h+sum_cross Lambda^0_ik Q_ik\n                      =V_(h_left)+Rbd||g_j||^2.         (21)\n\nFor an explicit coefficient check before the f_j=0 shift, V_h-V_left\nhas value coefficient2(P+b)f_j and gradient coefficient\nb(1-b)+P(P+1) on ||g_j||^2. The ideal cross sum contributes\nq(d-B)f_j+q[B/2-d(b-1/2)]||g_j||^2. The value coefficients cancel\nbecause q(B-d)=2(A+d)=2(P+b). The remaining coefficient is\n\n    A^2-(1+q)d^2\n      =[(A+d)(AB-d(A+B+d))]/(B-d)=Rbd.\n\nThe actual prefix inequalities Q_ik<=0 and Lambda_ik>=0 make their\ncontribution in(1) nonpositive. Every cross residual on these formal\ndata has absolute value at most(2P+b+1/2)Gmax^2: actual convexity gives\n|f_i-f_j|<=Gmax||x_i-x_j||<=P Gmax^2, GD gives\n||x_i-x_j||<=P Gmax, and the formulas are\n\n    Q_(i,k)=f_i+<g_i,-b g_j-x_i>+||g_i||^2/2,\n    Q_(k,i)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2,                    i<=j<k.\n\nEquations(1),(15),(21) and full M>=0 now give(20).\nThis proof uses the unobserved formal extension only inside a PSD\nidentity; it never claims those data constitute a legal GD witness.\n\nThe error Rbd is linear in the scalar cut gap:\n\n    delta=Delta_cut(AB+Cd)/(B+d)^2,\n    Rbd=delta (A+d)(B+d)^2/[(B-d)(AB+Cd)].              (22)\n\nConsequently, along a bounded positive-step family with m and d bounded\naway from0, B-d bounded away from0, and all beta_i bounded, the right\nside's coefficient in(20) is O(sqrt(delta)). This asymptotic notation\nis only a consequence of the explicit bounds(14),(22).\n\nAn actual prefix witness with V_left=-nu Gmax^2, nu>0, therefore forces\n\n    nu<=Rbd+(2P+b+1/2)Lbd.                              (23)\n\nThis is an explicit necessary condition on the parent steps. It supplies\na quantitative route from a prefix obstruction to a positive gap at the\nparent cut, rather than assuming an exactly saturated cut.\n\nFor a concrete symbolic instance, take a parent whose first step is3/2\nand select j=1, assuming its selected bridge h_1>1 and all steps positive.\nFor its one-step prefix, f(x)=x^2/2 started at1 has gradients1,-1/2,\nGmax=1 and V_left=1-(5/2)^2(1/2)^2=-9/16. Therefore every such parent\nfull certificate must satisfy\n\n    Rbd+(b+7/2)Lbd>=9/16.                              (24)\n\nIn any bounded family meeting the positivity conditions after(22),\nthis prohibits approaching delta=0 with that fixed first step. It does\nnot exclude a parent with a positive cut gap or any unrelated seed.\n\n## 6. Equality, zero steps, and exact symbolic checks\n\nAt delta=0, every quantity in(14) vanishes and Delta_cut=0. Thus all\ncross entries are exactly(13). Their mixed gradient block is zero by\n(18),(19). The left internal multipliers are nonnegative and give a\nfull certificate for V_left, since(21) now has Rbd=0 and the full left\nmatrix is the corresponding principal PSD block. This recovers the\nold saturated-prefix restriction by a new limiting-compatible estimate.\n\nWriting kappa=(C/B)^2 at equality gives q=kappa-1. The accepted full\nprimitive-join identity then shows that the tail has a certificate for\n\n    V_tail-(1-1/kappa)sum_(k<r)h_k Q_(k,r),\n\nwhere this sum is over nonterminal TAIL indices. Removing this signed\ncredit would subtract q h_k from its actual coefficient Lambda_(k,r).\nNothing in the preceding estimates asserts that those differences are\nnonnegative. Thus no automatic ordinary primitive-tail restriction is\ninferred; the old counterexample remains relevant.\n\nZero steps require care. The formulas(1),(2),(6) at a POSITIVE cut hold\neven if other steps vanish. But(7)--(15) for every coordinate are not\nclaimed for an original word containing zeros. A full certificate can\nhave arbitrarily large curvature in an unused zero-step gradient direction.\nFor example h=(0), Lambda_01=Lambda_10=t>=0 satisfies the complete\nflow equations and has M=t[[1,-1],[-1,1]]. Its jump matrix is[t], with\nno finite step-only bound on t. This is an exact counterexample to\nextending beta=2-b^2 continuously to a formal coordinate at b=0.\n\nFor necessary schedule tests one may instead compress zero steps in a\njustified way. Retain the original index of each positive step and the\noriginal terminal index. Identify formal jets within each repeated-query\ngroup only to RESTRICT a given full certificate. The GD formulas and\nV_h become exactly those of the positive-step compressed word. An edge\nwithin one group vanishes after restriction; every other edge becomes\nthe corresponding compressed Q with its nonnegative coefficients summed.\nThus a full original certificate implies a full compressed certificate.\n\nConversely, a full compressed primitive certificate embeds into the\noriginal formal space by assigning its edges only to the retained indices\nand assigning zero to other edges. Zero steps contribute nothing to V_h\nor to any queried displacement, and the original terminal is retained.\nTherefore the embedded identity is the pullback of the compressed PSD\nmatrix and is PSD on ALL original independent gradients. No equality\nbetween zero-step gradients is needed for this converse. The all-zero\nword has the zero certificate. This proves feasibility equivalence while\nalso explaining why the original matrix entries need not be bounded.\n\nIn particular the tests above may be applied to the compressed word,\nand the exact saturated-prefix conclusion may be lifted to the original\nprefix by this embedding. The quantitative constants for a compressed\nword refer to its compressed coefficient array, not an unjustified bound\non every possible original zero-step coefficient.\n\nFor one positive step b, the only value-flow and constant-kernel solution\nis Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=2b+2. Direct expansion gives\n\n    V_(b)+2Q_01+(2b+2)Q_10=(2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.\n\nBoth versions(8),(9) reduce exactly to beta_0=2-b^2. Thus they recover\nthe complete one-step interface and its endpoint b=sqrt(2), including\nthe b<1 branch, without a numerical evaluation.\n\n## 7. Scope and remaining missing object\n\nFor the C78 problem set r=4 and require sum h_i>=168/25, retaining all20\noriginal nonnegative multipliers and the full5-gradient matrix. The new\nconstraints(6),(12),(15),(20) are necessary for that complete interface.\nThey complement scalar cut bounds and can support exact rejection or\nreconstruction of a proposed seed. No candidate word has been evaluated\nnumerically here; no witness with the required capacity has been found,\nand no separator for the entire feasible set has been established.\n\nThe original target remains open. The sole accepted scalar/composition\nbridge is fact04219554e41d9750. This draft has no verifier allocation and\nmay not be used as a fact. The final-cut equality subgoal in the round23\nplan was already present in shared work; the positive-slack estimates,\nsmall-step trajectories and zero-coordinate warning are the additions.\n\nJustification: explicit actual-function construction, coefficient\nidentities and elementary PSD inequalities. Main fragility checks are\nthe direction of cross residuals, small-step plateau legality, the full\noff-diagonal signs, and the distinction between a formal extension and\nan actual trajectory. Each is expanded above. an upper bound for (K_jump)_jj.", "Drow_i": "sqrt(beta_(i-1))+sqrt(beta_i), with beta_(-1)=beta_r=0, bounding sqrt(M_ii) for positive words.", "Delta_cut": "AB-Cd for the selected cut; A=1+prefix sum,B=1+suffix sum,d=bridge step minus1,C=1+total sum.", "t_jk": "Nonnegative cross-residual weight d+v(1+P_k-P_(j+1)) of the exact two-plateau trajectory.", "Lambda^0": "Comparison cross array with entries Lambda^0_(j,r)=d q and Lambda^0_(k,j)=q c_k, q=2(A+d)/(B-d).", "Fbd": "w beta_j/(a m), or zero for an empty suffix, bounding forward cross coefficients to nonterminal suffix indices.", "Bbd": "w beta_j/m, or zero for an empty prefix, bounding backward cross coefficients to indices before the bridge.", "Ubd": "Explicit bound in equation14 on forward edges from strict prefix indices to the terminal.", "Ebd": "Explicit sum of the approximate cross-coefficient equations' errors in equation14.", "Lbd": "Explicit bound in equation14 on the full cross array's l1 distance from Lambda^0.", "Rbd": "Nonnegative prefix residual (A+d)Delta_cut/(B-d) in the exact formal zero-tail identity.", "Gmax": "Maximum norm of the actual gradients in the prefix trajectory, including its endpoint.", "zeta": "Point strictly below the sampled small-step suffix, used to attach a zero-gradient region and hence a minimizer."}}
{"id": "0f7b7fbf61c684a0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T03:39:56.404317+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C79 complete joint four-step full-cone model and calibration draft: exact flow/PSD primitive identity, forced constant-gradient kernel, lossless two-way zero-step certificate compression, legal single-cut/curvature filters, exact rational ConPP certificate and legal nonprimitive calibration. Full nonlocal discovery found no seed; its proposal-count overrun is separately disclosed and all numerical work is closed. No global impossibility or new accepted fact.", "evidence": "# C79: four-step full-cone discovery and exact interfaces\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint research draft. Wave8, cumulative round18.\nNo fact_submit is authorized. All new algebra below is derived jointly;\nolder shared drafts are orientation, never correctness premises.\nThe accepted interpolation fact1252b8823bb5a290 is the only fact used.\n\n## Primitive energy and the full cone\n\nNormalize smoothness to1. For a nonnegative word h of length n, put\ns=sum h_i, A=s+1, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, T_i=sum_(t>i)h_t and\nx_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. Define\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                      -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nLet Lambda be the full nonnegative array on distinct finite indices0..n,\nwith zero diagonal. Set e=(1,...,1)^T, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\nT_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i, and\n\n omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,\n D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)).\n\nExpansion of the value coefficients gives L^T e=omega, and expansion of\nthe gradient coefficients gives\n\n V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>,\n M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.                                      (1)\n\nFor example the position part of sum Lambda Q has matrix\nsym(L(T-I)); the squared-gradient differences contribute\nsym(L)-diag(omega)/2. Adding the displayed diagonal of V gives (1).\nConsequently the original full interface is exactly flow, Lambda>=0 and\nM>=0. It is sufficient for V>=0 on every actual run, by Q_ij<=0 from\naccepted1252b8823bb5a290. Every original finite gradient is retained.\n\nFlow alone forces e^T M e=0: Te has entries1+P_i (P_n=s), and\ne^T LTe=omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2, whereas\ne^T De=-s^2-sum h_i^2. PSD therefore forces Me=0. Indeed for any v,\npositivity of (e+t v)^T M(e+t v) for both signs of small t forces\nv^T Me=0. Thus the exact dual formulation may impose Me=0 and maximize\nt subject to M-t(I-ee^T/(n+1))>=0. Its t>=0 feasibility is precisely the\noriginal cone. This removes only the proved constant-gradient kernel;\nno quadratic equality, rank, ordering, symmetry or sparsity is assumed.\n\nThe companion full primal minimizes V subject to Gram(g)>=0,\ntrace(Gram(g))=1, f_n=0 and every original ordered Q<=0. Its value is\nalways <=0: a constant-gradient Huber run gives V=0. Every negative\nfeasible transcript can be lifted to a legal function with a minimizer.\nTo see this, append the coordinate\ng'_i=(g_i,epsilon), x'_i=(x_i,R-epsilon P_i),\nf'_i=f_i+c-epsilon^2 P_i, with epsilon>0. Then Q'_ij=Q_ij.\nChoose finite c and R so\n\n c>=max_i(-f_i+epsilon^2 P_i+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2),\n epsilon R>=max_i(f_i+c-<g_i,x_i>+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2).\n\nThese inequalities add the zero optimum jet in both orientations. The\nconjugate construction of accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the actual\nconvex1-smooth function. The additional V is epsilon^2 times\n2sum h_i(s-P_i)+sum h_i(1-h_i)-s(s+1)=0, since\n2sum h_i P_i=s^2-sum h_i^2. No distance-preservation claim is made.\n\n## Zero steps: exact two-way cone correspondence\n\nPartition the query indices into maximal consecutive classes separated\nby positive steps. In each class except the last, choose the index of\nthe positive outgoing step as representative; choose n in the last.\nLet hhat be the ordered list of positive steps. Embedding any compressed\ncertificate on these representatives gives the original V and Q on\ntheir independent formal gradients. All omitted coefficients are zero,\nso its full matrix is the coordinate-selection congruence of the\ncompressed PSD matrix. This proves the forward embedding.\n\nConversely, restrict an original certificate to formal gradients and\nvalues that are equal within each class. Its V becomes V_hhat; each\ncross-class Q becomes the corresponding compressed Q; each within-class\nQ is zero. Sum all original nonnegative multipliers on each ordered\npair of distinct classes. This yields compressed nonnegative multipliers,\nthe compressed flow, and a PSD matrix by congruence. Thus certificate\nexistence is equivalent in both directions, including all-zero schedules\n(V=0, Lambda=0). No numerical model below uses this compression: all\nfive original gradients are still saved and optimized.\n\n## Necessary filters proved from legal functions\n\nFor j with b=h_j>1, write P=P_j, R=T_j and a=(b-1)/(R+1)>0.\nUse the one-dimensional convex1-smooth function whose derivative is\nclip(x,-a,1), with minimum0, and start at x_0=P+1. Every query through\nj is on the upper plateau, the bridge lands at -(R+1)a, and all later\nqueries remain on the lower plateau until x_n=-a. At every query\nf_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, equivalently Q_(i,star)=0. Summing the GD norm\nidentity and substituting this equality into V yields\n\n V_h=(P+1)^2-A^2 a^2.\n\nThus a necessary condition for the cone is\n\n A(h_j-1)_+ <= (P_j+1)(T_j+1), for every j.                (2)\n\nFor h_j<=1 this is automatic. At j=0 or n-1, (2) gives\nh_j<=2(s+1)/(s+2)<2 when h_j>1. Also, for h_j>1,\nh_j-1 <=(P_j+1)(T_j+1)/A<=min(P_j+1,T_j+1), because\nA=P_j+T_j+h_j+1 >=max(P_j+1,T_j+1).\nAt n4 this bounds h_1,h_2<4 using the adjacent endpoint bound.\nSo [0,2]x[0,4]x[0,4]x[0,2] is a lossless closed outer box.\nIt does not imply all points of the box, or all points satisfying (2),\nare primitive. In particular no upper sum below12 is claimed.\n\nFor curvature z in(0,1], use f(x)=z x^2/2, x_0=1, and\np_0(z)=1, p_(i+1)(z)=(1-z h_i)p_i(z). Direct substitution gives\n\n V_h(z)=sum_i z h_i(1+z-z h_i)p_i(z)^2\n                      -s z[1+(s+1)z]p_n(z)^2 >=0.        (3)\n\nAt z=1 this becomes 1-A^2 product_i(1-h_i)^2>=0 by telescoping.\nEach fixed z gives a necessary test only. The implementation records\nz=1,1/2,1/4; it performs no hidden curvature or word scan.\n\n## Exact calibrations\n\nThe word (1,2,1,1) is the ConPP join of (1) and (1,1): their masses are\n2 and3, the bridge increment d=1 solves d(2+3+d)=2*3, and the physical\nbridge is1+d=2. Independently of any external composition theorem, its\ncomplete nonzero certificate entries are\n\n 01:2, 10:4, 21:3, 31:3, 41:3, 14:3,\n 23:8, 32:16, 34:35, 43:48, 24:3.\n\nSubstitution in (1) gives exactly\nM=(e_0-e_1)(e_0-e_1)^T+4(e_2-e_3)(e_2-e_3)^T\n                               +16(e_3-e_4)(e_3-e_4)^T.\nThe child certificates are lambda01=2,lambda10=4,M=(e0-e1)^2\nfor (1), and lambda01=2,lambda10=4,lambda12=8,lambda21=12,\nM=(e0-e1)^2+4(e1-e2)^2 for (1,1).\nThus the exact primitive primal minimum is0, attained by constant jets.\n\nFor (3,1,1,1), use x=(1,-2,-3/2,-1,-1/2),\ng=(1,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2), f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.\nThese are samples of the same clipped-gradient function with slopes\n[-1/2,1]. They give V=-45/4 and sum g_i^2=2, hence the full primal\nminimum is at most -45/8. All20 original interpolation inequalities\nand both optimum orientations are obeyed. This fixes the expected sign.\n\nAn additional exact length4 zero-remainder ConPP calibration is built\nrecursively from the empty word: join children a,b of masses A,C using\nd>0 satisfying d(A+C+d)=AC, k=((A+C+d)/C)^2, and h=(a,1+d,b).\nFor bridge query p and terminal query z, the exact identity is\n\n V_h=V_a+k V_b-(k-1)[sum_(j in b nonterminal)b_j Q_(j,p)\n                 +Q_(z,p)+d Q_(p,z)+sum_j b_j Q_(j,z)].  (4)\n\nIt follows by expanding Q and using (k-1)(C-d)=2(A+d),\nk d^2=A^2 and k C^2=(A+C+d)^2; C>d>0 gives every actual sign.\nEach recursive child retains all its original indices. The baseline\nuses a=join(empty,empty) and b=join(empty,a), then join(a,b).\nThe primitive calibration is (1), (4) and its explicit multiplier array,\nnot an ordinary objective-gap claim. Source terminology was checked in\nZhang--Jiang, arXiv2410.12395v2, Definition2.7 and Theorem3.1 proof;\nthe displayed identities are independently rederived here.\n\n## Numerical scope and accounting\n\nThe authorized new ledger is max3_four_step_seed_discovery_budget.json:\n128 charged model/optimization evaluations,120 measured build-plus-solve\nseconds,96 distinct nonbaseline proposals,n4 only. Every proposal is\nregistered before its evaluation, including filtered or failed proposals;\nall original data and source hashes are saved. Old closed ledgers are\nneither imported as executable runners nor modified.\n\nThe search uses all four physical step coordinates with s>=168/25,\nthe lossless box, necessary (2)-(3), and full primal/dual sensitivities.\nSeveral distant initializations are explored adaptively; neither the sum\nnor the rank nor the order is fixed as a global reduction. A local\nnon-discovery is not global nonexistence. Numerical near-zero, success\nstatus, a clipped eigenvalue or PSD repair is never an exact seed.\nAny actual result must satisfy the original flow, all20 signs, full PSD\nand sum threshold exactly; C78's separate audit must also be checked.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_c79_four_step_full_cone.md", "handoff": "research/max3_c79_c80_round18_handoff.md", "finding_ids": ["6f57ab3197b4cc9d", "bf740a9c1acf0955", "7b447329aac48edd"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "04219554e41d9750"], "attempt_type": "direct", "status": "full_model_calibrated_finite_non_discovery", "new_verifier_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"V_h": "Primitive intrinsic energy defined in the attached joint draft and accepted04219554e41d9750.", "Lambda_ij": "All nonnegative ordered finite interpolation multipliers, including both terminal directions.", "M_prim": "Full primitive remainder D+sym(LT), denoted M locally."}}

It retains every auxiliary negative curvature residual, derives the exact maximum repair weight, checks every original ordered pair and optimum pair, and uses accepted1252b8823bb5a290 to obtain one actual function. For four-step middle cuts its complete continuum is a piecewise polynomial condition of degrees at most5 and7. It uses no unverified shared finding as a premise.\n\n(4) Full-domain closure remains STUCK. First direct route: even after C81 parameter elimination, the remaining inequalities contain the four schedule parameters and have no proved converse to the full twenty-sign flow/PSD cone. Second direct route: C83 removes the curvature continuum to explicit polynomial branches, but the branch comparison polynomials depend on all four schedule parameters; no proof that every schedule above the threshold violates one branch was obtained. The existing compact/all-h_i>1 reductions are UNVERIFIED shared drafts, not accepted facts used here. No new candidate family was scanned to assume the residual semialgebraic region empty.\n\nCounterexample check of the strongest possible closure claim is INCONCLUSIVE: exact record100 refutes its specific primitive-candidate claim, while C80 already refutes cut-plus-all-quadratic sufficiency; neither is a counterexample to, or proof of, sufficiency of the enlarged combined C80/C82/C83 family. (1)\n\nThus even all these freedoms cannot make this specific geometric family\nreach one quarter of the ORIGINAL function-gap benchmark. A positive combination\nof three cycles of ORIGINAL queried inequalities is strictly greater than\n22/25 times a positive receiving energy. All free values cancel exactly.\nThis extends the retained-geometry exclusion to simultaneous changes of ALL\ntransverse maps and ALL function values. It does not change the atom or allow\nrelative translations of children. No claim is made that every free-map63\ngeometry is impossible; the earlier fixed-first-map63 draft had a different\nrestriction and is not used here.\n\n(III) Relative translations genuinely change the model. It disproves the proposed shortcut\nthat zero-residual child endpoints force every relative translation to vanish.\nIt supplies no target-relevant63 construction or uniform large-horizon family.\n\nThese statements concern one static global function for each given horizon.\nThere is no alteration of a function or values during a GD run. The original\nall-schedule asymptotic target remains unresolved.\n\n## 2. Atom, physical words, and every allowed isometry\n\nUse s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1=1/lambda and\n\n  a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300, rho=599/600, eps=1/100,\n  Klen=a+rho, z_atom=b-1, eta=z_atom/Klen, m=a+b,\n  A=1+2a+b=m+a+1.\n\nIn R^3 define\n\n  U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho*sqrt(eta),eps),\n  (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(U,U,V,W),\n  (x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)=(mU+aV+W,bU+aV+W,aV+W,W).\n\nThe atomic word is h[0]=(a,b,a). Its initial vector is p=x_0. Define\n\n  R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Klen*z_atom+eps^2,\n  N=||W||^2=rho^2*eta+eps^2,\n  D=<p,W>=rho*z_atom+eps^2,\n  kappa=A^2*N/R^2,\n  y_1=z_atom*U+Klen*V+eps*(0,0,1),\n  y_2=(a-1)*V+W,\n  e=<V,y_2>=(Klen-1)*eta,\n  d=(1-rho)*e-eps^2=e/600-eps^2. (3)\n\nLet h[j+1]=(h[j],1+r_j,h[j]), A_j=lambda^j*A and\nr_j=theta*A_j. These are the actual physical words: h[j] has length\nn_j=4*2^j-1 and 1+sum h[j]=A_j, because 2+theta=lambda.\n\nAt each internal node with two depth-j children, let P_R be the right initial\nvector and w_L the left endpoint. Free heights: exact original inequalities and their price\n\nTake ANY actual convex1-smooth function realizing the specified queried\ngeometry and possessing a minimizer z. Subtract its minimum from all values,\nso f_star=0. The geometric origin need not be z. For a queried sample define\n\n  aheight_i=f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2,\n  y_i=x_i-g_i,\n  N_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.\n\nDirect expansion of the ORIGINAL convention in1252b8823bb5a290 gives\n\n  Q_ij=aheight_i-aheight_j+N_ij,\n  Q_(i,star)=aheight_i+<g_i,z>,\n  Q_(star,i)=-aheight_i-<g_i,y_i>.                            (9)\n\nAll must be nonpositive. If two queried samples have residual zero,\ntheir two original pair rows force their heights to be equal. Call the\ncommon value aheight_common. Their star lower rows also give aheight_common>=0.\n\nIn an atomic leaf scaled by gamma, whose endpoint is queried, the rows\nfrom each sample into that endpoint give aheight_i<=aheight_common. The reverse path from the\nendpoint through samples2,1,0, using (3), gives\n\n  aheight_common-gamma^2*d<=aheight_2<=aheight_common,\n  aheight_common-gamma^2*d<=aheight_0=aheight_1<=aheight_common.                             (10)\n\nIndeed Q_(3,2)=aheight_common-aheight_2-gamma^2*d<=0,\nQ_(2,1)=aheight_2-aheight_1<=0, and Q_(1,0)=aheight_1-aheight_0<=0.\nThe reverse row Q_(0,1) gives aheight_0=aheight_1 because those gradients coincide.\nEvery row used is inside the queried prefix; no free terminal row is used.\n\nConsider ANY entirely queried7-step subtree scaled by gamma. Its exact\nfirst-bridge vector identity is\n\n  r_0*g_3 + m*g_4 + a*g_6 + g_7=0.                    (11)\n\nHere the local indices refer to this subtree. Its right leaf has scale\ngamma*alpha_0. Multiply the star upper rows in(9) by r_0,m,a,1 and add.\nTheir z terms cancel by(11). Applying(10) to the right leaf yields\n\n  (A+r_0)*aheight_common<=(A-1)*gamma^2*alpha_0^2*d.\n\nThus define the exact height price\n\n  K_height=((A-1)/(A+r_0))*alpha_0^2*d;\n  0<=aheight_common<=gamma^2*K_height.                              (12)\n\nThis is forced by actual optimum rows and actual internal interpolation.\nIt is not an imposed value prescription. As A+r_0=sqrt(2)*A,\nA<6 and sqrt(2)>7/5 give\n\n  (A-1)/(A+r_0)<25/42,\n  K_height< (25/42)*(1/4)*(1/1000)=1/6720.             (13)\n\nThere is a simultaneous cost for changing the minimizer. In the first\natomic leaf, p=m*g_0+a*g_2+g_3. Multiply its three star upper rows by\nm,a,1 and use(10):\n\n  -<p,z> >= A*aheight_common-(A-1)*d.\n\nSince the initial vector is p up to an isometry,\n\n  ||x_0-z||^2\n     >=R^2+2*A*aheight_common-2*(A-1)*d+||z||^2\n     >R^2-1/100.                                      (14)\n\nThis holds for EVERY minimizer z, including components outside the child\nspan. Hence it bounds the distance to the entire minimizer set. Its infimum\nstill exceeds R^2-1/100 because the stronger uniform bound\nR^2-2*(A-1)*d already has a strict positive gap from that value.\n\nFinally, take any queried zero-residual anchor u=x_i=g_i. Smoothness at that\nactual sample gives, for every x in the ambient space,\n\n  f(x)<=f_i+<u,x-u>+||x-u||^2/2=||x||^2/2+aheight_common.          (15)\n\nThis bound controls a FREE final gradient as well as a prescribed one.\nIt follows from an actual queried jet; it deletes no incoming query row.\n\n## 5. Applying the price to the ORIGINAL31-step function gap\n\nThe depth3 word has queried samples0,...,30. Its subtree at indices16,...,23\nis an entirely queried7-step child scaled by gamma=alpha_2. Its endpoints19\nand23 are queried and have residual zero. Equations(8),(12),(13) give\n\n  0<=aheight_common<1/26880.                                        (16)\n\nThe final position has squared norm N_3 even if the actual final gradient is\nchosen freely. By(15), 2(f(x_31)-min f)<=N_3+2*aheight_common. Equations(5),(8) give\n\n  N_3/R^2=kappa^8/A_3^2<3/12800.\n\nFrom accepted b98bb807d514e80d, writing p_exp=log_2(3),\n\n  B_31<=1+4*31^p_exp<1+4*32^p_exp=973.                 (17)\n\nBy(6),(14), the true initial squared distance exceeds R^2-1/100, and\n\n  R^2/(R^2-1/100)<1001/1000,\n  R^2-1/100>697/50.\n\nConsequently the contribution of the geometric endpoint norm to the\nB_31-normalized gap is less than\n\n  (2919/12800)*(1001/1000)<229/1000,\n\nwhere 2919*1001=2921919<2931200=229*12800. The contribution of the\nfree common height is less than\n\n  973/(13440*(697/50))=48650/9367680<6/1000,\n\nwhere 48650000<56206080. Adding proves (1), with no assumption on the actual\nterminal gradient, its span, or the position of any minimizer. All other\ninterpolation rows can only narrow this family, so no omitted row can\ninvalidate this conditional performance upper bound.\n\nThis is NOT an estimate of W_31. It says these particular queried vectors\ncannot produce a strong witness, even after values and normalization are\noptimized. The earlier actual31 projection realization remains only a draft\ncalibration; this proof does not use its feasibility as a premise.\n\n## 6. A convex sender independent of EVERY first coupling\n\nNow consider a purported depth5,127-step realization. Its two depth4\nchildren have63 steps each. Denote the initial vector of the right child by\nP, and its first atomic endpoint by W_first, with ||P||=R and\n<P,W_first>=D. Inside its first7 subtree, the exact bridge relation gives\n\n  m*G_4+a*G_6+G_7=-r_0*W_first.\n\nThe coefficients (m,a,1)/A are positive and sum to1, so\n\n  v=(m*G_4+a*G_6+G_7)/A=-theta*W_first,\n  q=-<P,v>/R^2=theta*D/R^2,\n  T_perp=||v-(<P,v>/R^2)*P||<=theta*sqrt(N).            (18)\n\nAll later left embeddings preserve these identities. They depend on no\ntransverse coupling, including the first. This is why a convex sender is\nneeded in place of the single G_4 from the older63 separator.\n\nPut beta=beta_4. The last atomic leaf of the LEFT63 child is an isometric\nimage scaled by beta. Its endpoint, receiving gradient and receiving\nresidual are respectively beta*W,beta*V,beta*y_2, up to that isometry.\nIts receiving energy is E_L=beta^2*e>0. The top bridge has\n\n  r=r_4=lambda^3*A,\n  alpha=alpha_4=r*beta*sqrt(N)/R.\n\nWrite h_angle=<W,y_2>=rho*e+eps^2. The component of y_2 perpendicular to W\nhas norm\n\n  ||y_2-(h_angle/N)W||=(a-1)*eps*sqrt(eta/N).           (19)\n\nTo prove(19), project (a-1)V perpendicular to W and use\n||V||^2-<V,W>^2/N=eta*eps^2/N.\n\nUnder the top isometries, define the weighted receiving normal\n\n  N_bar=<alpha*Rmap(v)-Lmap(beta*V), Lmap(beta*y_2)>.\n\nThe bridge sends P to minus the endpoint direction. Its axial contribution\nto the first inner product is r*beta^2*h_angle*q. The transverse contribution\nis bounded below by Cauchy--Schwarz and(19), in the ACTUAL common space.\nSubtracting the receiving energy gives\n\n  N_bar/E_L >= r*H_angle-1,\n  H_angle=(rho+eps^2/e)*q\n     -((a-1)*eps*sqrt(eta)/(R*e))*T_perp.              (20)\n\nThis is a bound on a geometric normal, not yet on a Q row with free values.\nThe exact value elimination is supplied in the next section.\n\nFrom theta>2/5 and(6),\n\n  q>99/3550, T_perp<1/3.\n\nThus the positive term in H_angle is greater than9801/355000; using\na-1<1/2, eps=1/100, sqrt(eta)<1, R>3, e>1/2 and T_perp<1/3,\nthe subtracted term is less than1/900. Hence\n\n  H_angle>9801/355000-1/900>1/40.                      (21)\n\nFor the last inequality, 1/40+1/900=47/1800 and\n9801*1800=17641800>16685000=47*355000.\nAlso r=lambda^3*A>80 by(8), so\n\n  N_bar/E_L>1.                                        (22)\n\n## 7. Three original cycles eliminate every free value\n\nGlobal indices64,...,127 belong to the right child. Its retained first-right\natomic leaf has indices68,...,71 and total scale alpha_4*alpha_0.\nUse the three queried cycles\n\n  C_68 = Q_(68,62)+Q_(62,63)+Q_(63,71)\n                         +Q_(71,70)+Q_(70,69)+Q_(69,68),\n  C_70 = Q_(70,62)+Q_(62,63)+Q_(63,71)+Q_(71,70),\n  C_71 = Q_(71,62)+Q_(62,63)+Q_(63,71).\n\nEvery index is at most71 and therefore queried at horizon127. The positive\nweights m/A,a/A,1/A sum to1. Define\n\n  S_cycle=(m/A)*C_68+(a/A)*C_70+(1/A)*C_71.             (23)\n\nIn each cycle the heights in(9) telescope. The receiving residuals at63\nand71 are zero, and the atomic return normals are exactly(3), scaled by\nalpha_4^2*alpha_0^2. Consequently\n\n  S_cycle=N_bar-((A-1)/A)*alpha_4^2*alpha_0^2*d.       (24)\n\nThere is no prescribed value in(24). It is an identity for arbitrary\nreal values at the displayed samples.\n\nBecause r_4*r_0=lambda^2*A^2, the price relative to E_L is\n\n  ((A-1)/A)*alpha_4^2*alpha_0^2*d/E_L\n    =((A-1)/A)*lambda^4*kappa^2*(d/e).\n\nHere d/e<1/600, lambda^2<6 and kappa<21/20<7/5. The price is therefore\nstrictly less than\n\n  36*(49/25)/600=1764/15000<3/25.                     (25)\n\nEquations(22),(24),(25) prove S_cycle>(22/25)*E_L>0. Each original Q in\nthis positive combination would have to be nonpositive for an actual\nsmooth-convex function. This is the desired contradiction. It uses neither\nthe final output nor an optimum, so terminal or minimizer freedom cannot\naffect it. No feasibility of an intermediate child was presumed.\n\nA common spatial scale c>0 multiplies every cycle normal and E_L by c^2.\nA common translation leaves the original cycle weights unchanged. Thus no\npositive-scale translated copy of the whole specified geometry escapes.\nAt c=0 the trivial constant-function face survives; a conic model including\nthat face must not be described as wholly infeasible.\n\n## 8. A genuinely different join: relative translations\n\nThis section is a direct screen of an uncovered change, not an assertion\nthat it repairs the actual63 instance. Take two fixed child transcripts with\ninitial point p_R and left endpoint x=g=w_L. Choose isometries Lmap,Rmap and\nan arbitrary right scale alpha>0. Put\n\n  u_i=Lmap(g_L,i), Y_i=Lmap(x_L,i-g_L,i),\n  v_j=alpha*Rmap(g_R,j), Z_j=alpha*Rmap(x_R,j-g_R,j),\n  t_join=-r*Lmap(w_L)-alpha*Rmap(p_R).\n\nUse actual positions Lmap(x_L,i) on the left and\nt_join+alpha*Rmap(x_R,j) on the right. The bridge1+r is exact, and all\ninternal GD updates are unchanged. Introduce intrinsic heights\n\n  aheight_L,i=f_L,i-<u_i,Lmap(x_L,i)>+||u_i||^2/2,\n  aheight_R,j=f_R,j-<v_j,alpha*Rmap(x_R,j)>+||v_j||^2/2.\n\nExpanding every original cross row gives exactly\n\n  Q_(L_i,R_j)=aheight_L,i-aheight_R,j+<u_i,t_join>+<u_i-v_j,Z_j>,\n  Q_(R_j,L_i)=aheight_R,j-aheight_L,i-<v_j,t_join>+<v_j-u_i,Y_i>. (26)\n\nWithin-child rows retain their intrinsic-height expressions. If queried\nendpoint anchors have intrinsic residual zero, their heights are common\nwithin each child, denoted aheight_L,aheight_R. Their cross rows are equivalent to\n\n  max_(left anchors u) <u,t_join>\n       <= aheight_R-aheight_L\n       <= min_(right anchors v) <v,t_join>.             (27)\n\nIn particular <u-v,t_join><=0 for each such pair. The direction in(27)\nmatters: reversing it would incorrectly exclude legal translated examples.\n\nFor an actual common minimizer z with value zero, the ORIGINAL star rows\nalso require\n\n  -<u_i,Y_i> <= aheight_L,i <= -<u_i,z>,\n  -<v_j,Z_j> <= aheight_R,j <= <v_j,t_join-z>.               (28)\n\nThe initial-distance bound is ||Lmap(p_L)-z||^2, and a free actual final\ngradient still needs all terminal rows (or the precisely matched completion\nfrom1252b8823bb5a290). Equations(26)-(28) price a relative translation; it is\nnot an arbitrary independent offset in the values or optimum.\n\n### A real counterexample to the claim that t_join must vanish\n\nFix r>0 and 0<q<r. Use two empty scalar children with intrinsic endpoint\nposition/gradient1. Take the left isometry as identity, the right as sign\nreversal and right scale alpha=q. The one physical step is h=1+r. The\nactual samples are\n\n  (x_0,g_0)=(1,1), (x_1,g_1)=(-r,-q), t_join=q-r!=0.\n\nPut\n\n  mu=(1+q)/(1+r), z=(q-r)/(1+q),\n  f(x)=(mu/2)*(x-z)^2.\n\nHere 0<mu<1, so this is one globally convex1-smooth function with unique\nminimizer z. Its gradients at the displayed points are1 and-q, and GD with\nstep1+r maps1 to-r. For any two samples, including z, its original residual\nis -mu*(1-mu)*(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0; hence all rows hold. The intrinsic heights are\n\n  aheight_L=(r-q)/(2*(1+q)), aheight_R=q^2*aheight_L,\n  aheight_R-aheight_L=(q-1)*(r-q)/2.\n\nThey satisfy the exact anchor interval\n-(r-q)<=aheight_R-aheight_L<=q*(r-q). The TRUE initial distance and payoff are\n\n  |x_0-z|=(1+r)/(1+q)=1/mu,\n  2(f(x_1)-min f)/|x_0-z|^2=q^2*mu.                   (29)\n\nThis pays the minimizer displacement explicitly. As a small illustration,\nr=1,q=1/2 gives z=-1/3, mu=3/4, distance4/3 and payoff3/16; the geometric\ndistance1 to the old origin would have given a different, incorrect\nnormalization. If both displayed endpoints must be QUERIED, append one step of size1\non this same quadratic: the displayed samples remain unchanged and both\nare queried in the longer run. One may also choose r=theta to match the\nbalanced bridge excess of two empty children. This example disproves\ntranslation impossibility only. It does not imply that the old multi-gradient children can be translated\ncompatibly, nor does it improve the all-function worst case of that word.\n\n### What the direct construction attempts do and do not establish\n\nKeeping the old opposite-source alignment and old right scale forces\nt_join=0 by the bridge identity, so changing only value constants cannot\nproduce the new mechanism. Dropping that alignment permits the scalar\nexample above. For a multigradient child, endpoint interval(27) alone does\nnot control the residual terms in(26), all of which remain required.\nWe have neither a compatible full63 translated realization with useful\nnormalized payoff, nor a separator for all such translated realizations.\n\nFor clarity, this is still a concrete finite model if pursued later. A full\nGram block for arbitrary scaled left/right frames is\n\n  [ t_L*I, S; S^T, t_R*I ] >=0,\n\nwhere I is the identity on each fixed child frame, t_L,t_R>=0 are its\nsquared scales, and S is the completely unrestricted cross block. Define t_join\nas the displayed linear combination of represented vectors, then append\narbitrary optimum and actual terminal vectors in ONE full PSD Gram, impose\nall original rows and ||x_0-z||^2<=1, and maximize2f_end. No guessed span or\nPSD range condition is discarded. The old source-matched model is the\nadditional vector equality t_join=0. This formulation has not been solved,\nand no numerical allowance is inferred from writing it down. Equations(1)\nand(23)-(25), rather than this open model, are the complete new results.\n\n## 9. Literature and handoff\n\nMatlas was queried twice for smooth convex integration, conjugate residuals,\nand cycle/convex-combination mechanisms; its hits were unrelated. Web search\nlocated Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, arXiv1502.05666. The exact already\ndownloaded paper downloads/1502.05666.txt was read at Theorem4 and proof,\nCorollary1, and Remarks1 and3. That literature confirms the relevant regime:\ncomplete smooth-convex interpolation, with values eliminated by all cycles.\nNo external theorem is imported into this proof;1252b8823bb5a290 already\nsupplies the exact original conditions and constructive sufficiency. Relative\ntranslations do not introduce a different function class or warrant a new\nliterature layer before the elementary screen above.\n\nThe two plans are dea316c4076eeb64 (all-map released cycles) and\nc1f744194935dbeb (translated joins). The first has a complete scoped joint\ncandidate, supplemented by the original-objective bound(1). The second has\nexact conditions and a real counterexample to an overstrong shortcut; its\ntarget-relevant construction remains open. Do not restart the excluded\nsource-matched family by retuning values, minimizers, final gradients or\ntransverse maps alone. A relative translation, changed queried atom, or\nanother actual joining rule is a substantive uncovered change, and must\nsatisfy all original rows and pay the original normalization.\n\nAll new claims remain unverified. No existing fact, shared truth store,\nclosed numerical ledger or proof gate was changed. {"id": "44fb89e3e8aa8c34", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T04:53:25.992128+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max4 round24 COMPLETE JOINT DRAFT: an explicit initial-to-terminal chord family has a genuinely variable full PSD remainder and all admissible signed-tail/skew freedom. Its entire full-matrix test reduces exactly to a tail PSD test plus one2x2 PSD block; a rational length4 nonzero-chord example is given. Nevertheless EVERY four-step member has capacity<=C0=Omega(1+sqrt2,Omega(1,1+sqrt2))<77/10<193/25, including curved and distant variations. The decisive scalar obstruction is C(a-1)x<=D. This is a family obstruction, not full-cone nonexistence; no seed or new fact.", "evidence": "# Exact initial-chord coefficient family and its capacity obstruction\n\nStatus: **UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate**, max4, cumulative round24.\nThe current TASK authorizes symbolic research but no new fact_submit,\nnumerical discovery, replay, installation, or publication. No new fact is\nclaimed. The accepted input is04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11, for the\noriginal full primitive matrix and ConPP identities. Every new assertion\nused below is derived in this document. Earlier drafts are not premises.\n\nThe result concerns a specified family of full coefficient arrays. It is\nnot a characterization of the full four-step cone. Within this family the\ntail's entire symmetric PSD remainder and every admissible skew completion\nare free; neither its rank nor its incoming-terminal signs are frozen.\nThe initial-to-terminal coefficient is also free. The family has an exact\nnonzero-chord rational example, but its four-step capacity cannot exceed\nthe exact non-dyadic ConPP baseline, which is below193/25.\n\n## 1. Original formal identity and the kernel used here\n\nFor a nonnegative word h of length n, put s=sum h_i, C=1+s,\nP_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, and retain every independent formal gradient g_0,...,g_n.\nUse the original convention\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                             -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nFor any REAL off-diagonal array lambda, not necessarily nonnegative, let\nL=diag(lambda*e)-lambda, where e is the all-ones column. Let T_ii=1,\nT_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i. Define\n\n omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,\n D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<n,-s(s+2)).\n\nThe value cancellation and full gradient remainder are exactly\n\n L^T e=omega,\n M=D_prim+sym(LT),\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>.                 (1)\n\nHere sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. These formulas are the accepted full identity,\nwhich is linear in the array and hence also valid for real signed arrays.\nThe position contribution is sym(L(T-I)), and the gradient-difference\ncontribution is sym(L)-diag(omega)/2; this also checks (1) directly.\n\nFlow alone gives e^T M e=0. Indeed\n\n e^T D_prim e=-s^2-sum h_i^2,\n e^T LT e=omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2,\n\nusing Te=(1+P_i)_i and 2sum h_i P_i=s^2-sum h_i^2.\nIf M is PSD, then Me=0: nonnegativity of (e+t v)^T M(e+t v)\nfor both signs of sufficiently small t forces v^T Me=0 for every v.\nThe individual nonterminal rows are therefore\n\n sum_j lambda_ij(P_j-P_i)=2h_i(1+P_i),  i<n.              (2)\n\nThe terminal row is sum_(j<n)lambda_nj(s-P_j)=2s(s+1).\nNo second kernel is assumed. Once Me=0, the quadratic is invariant under\nsubtracting the same vector from every formal gradient.\n\nFor a nonnegative array and h_0=a>0, (2) also proves a<2 whenever s>0.\nThe first outgoing mass is positive, because sum_j lambda_0j P_j=2a.\nThe first diagonal is a(2-a)+(1-a)sum_j lambda_0j. It is strictly negative\nif a>=2, contradicting PSD. We will need only this weak endpoint bound.\n\n## 2. A freely variable signed tail and the full coefficient construction\n\nLet a>0, x>=0, and let b=(b_0,...,b_(m-1)) be any nonnegative tail.\nSet\n\n A=1+a, D=1+sum b_i, C=A+D+x,\n R=D+x, H=A+x, delta=AD-Cx,\n k=C^2/D^2, t=2CH/(DR), gamma=2k-2-t,\n sigma=H*delta/(D^2 R).                                  (3)\n\nThus the actual parent word is h=(a,1+x,b), its capacity is C, and\nC-1=a+R. Indices0,1 are the initial query and bridge query. Tail local\nindices i=0,...,m correspond to original indices i+2; write z=m+2 for\nthe original terminal index. Define weights w_i=b_i for i<m and w_m=1,\nso sum_i w_i=D.\n\nTake ANY real tail array ell that has value cancellation for V_b and a\nFULL PSD tail remainder M_b. Require ell_ij>=0 on every edge other than\nthe incoming-terminal edges i<m,j=m. Those incoming entries can be\nnegative, subject only to the actual parent signs below. All formal tail\ngradients, including at zero steps, remain independent. By Section1,\nM_b has the constant-gradient kernel.\n\nChoose a parameter Z>=0 and set\n\n mu=2-a^2+(a-1)R Z/a.\n\nDefine the following COMPLETE parent array; all entries not listed are0:\n\n lambda_01 = 2-(C-1)Z/a,\n lambda_10 = 2A-R Z/a,\n lambda_0z = Z,\n lambda_1z = 2AH/R-Z,\n lambda_(i+2,1) = t w_i,                     0<=i<=m,\n lambda_(i+2,j+2) = k ell_ij\n                   +gamma*b_i*1_(i<m,j=m),  i!=j.        (4)\n\nAll indices in (4) are original indices. If m=0, the sole tail gradient\nis the terminal one, and there are no within-tail entries. In the actual\nfour-step application m=2 and there are five formal gradients.\n\nThe exact sign conditions are\n\n 2-(C-1)Z/a>=0,  2A-R Z/a>=0,  2AH/R-Z>=0,\n k ell_(i,m)+gamma b_i>=0 for every i<m,                 (5)\n\ntogether with Z>=0 and the nonnegative nonterminal tail entries already\nspecified. Every remaining listed coefficient is automatically\nnonnegative, because k,t>0 and\n\n gamma=2H[D^2+(C+D)x]/(D^2 R)>=0.\n\nIn particular (5) retains the exact lower bound on each signed incoming\ntail coefficient, instead of replacing it by ordinary tail primitivity.\n\nWrite\n\n U=g_0-g_1,\n J=sum_(i<m)b_i g_(i+2)+g_z-Dg_1.\n\nThe full formal identity for (4) is\n\n V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n   = k<M_b,Gram(g_2,...,g_z)>\n       +mu||U||^2-Z<U,J>+sigma||J||^2.                  (6)\n\nConsequently, under the stated tail hypotheses and (5), (4) is a full\nnonnegative primitive certificate IF AND ONLY IF\n\n             [ mu,   -Z/2 ]\n             [ -Z/2, sigma ] >=0.                     (7)\n\nThis is equivalent to mu>=0, sigma>=0 and Z^2<=4mu*sigma.\nThe equivalence is on the whole original formal gradient space.\n\n### Complete algebraic check of (6)\n\nThe relations needed for flow and row moments are\n\n 2k=2+t+gamma,\n gamma D-tx=2H,\n tD=2CH/R,\n C=A+R=D+H.                                            (8)\n\nAt node0, outgoing minus incoming is\n2-(C-1)Z/a+Z-(2A-RZ/a)=-2a.\nAt node1 it is 2a+Z+(2AH/R-Z)-tD=-2(1+x).\nAt a nonterminal tail node it is\n(-2k+gamma+t)b_i=-2b_i.\nThe terminal flow then follows by summing all flows.\n\nFor the kernel, node0 has moment\na lambda_01+(C-1)lambda_0z=2a.\nNode1 has moment -a lambda_10+R lambda_1z=2A(1+x).\nFor a tail index with local prefix q_i=sum_(j<i)b_j, its within-tail\nmoment plus its edge to node1 is\n\n 2k b_i(1+q_i)+gamma b_i(D-1-q_i)\n                       -t b_i(1+x+q_i)\n   =2b_i(1+H+q_i),\n\nby (8). This is (2) at its original prefix P_(i+2)=H+q_i.\nThus every nonterminal row of Me vanishes. Flow gives e^TMe=0, so the\nterminal row vanishes too. This calculation does not assume (7).\n\nIt now suffices to compute after setting g_1=0. Let\nG=sum_(i<m)b_i g_(i+2) and q=g_z. The coefficient of ||g_0||^2 is\n\n a(2-a)+(1-a)(lambda_01+Z)=mu,\n\nand the only cross term involving g_0 and tail gradients is\n-Z<g_0,G+q>. For the tail-only part, subtract its supplied identity\nk M_b. The gamma sum of incoming-tail Q and the t sum of tail-to-bridge\nQ cancel all coefficients of sum b_i(1-b_i)||g_(i+2)||^2, by\n1-k+(gamma+t)/2=0. Their remaining coefficients of ||G||^2 and\n<G,q> are (t-gamma)/2 and t-gamma. The remaining ||q||^2 coefficient is\n\n -(C-1)C+k(D-1)D\n      +[t+(2AH/R-Z)+Z+gamma(D-1)]/2\n   =(t-gamma)/2.\n\nFor this equality, use kD^2=C^2 and 2AH/R=tD-2H.\nFinally\n\n (t-gamma)/2=t-(k-1)=H(AD-Cx)/(D^2R)=sigma.\n\nThis proves (6) with g_1=0. The already checked constant kernel restores\nthe displayed U,J form for arbitrary g_1.\n\nSufficiency of (7) follows from (6) and M_b PSD. For necessity, set every\ntail gradient equal to one freely chosen vector v. Its tail quadratic is\nzero. Then J=D(v-g_1) and U=g_0-g_1 can be varied independently, so full\nPSD forces precisely (7). This argument does not delete a formal\ncoordinate or prescribe equality in the statement of the certificate.\n\n## 3. A nonzero-chord rational four-step certificate\n\nFor a=1, x=0 and b=(1,1), take the tail array\n\n ell_01=2, ell_10=4, ell_12=8, ell_21=12,\n\nwith other entries0. Expanding (1) gives\nM_b=||g_2-g_3||^2+4||g_3-g_4||^2 in original indices.\nChoose Z=1/2. Then C=5, k=25/9, t=20/9, gamma=4/3,\nmu=1 and sigma=4/9. The complete nonzero parent array is\n\n 10:5/2, 04:1/2, 14:13/6,\n 21:20/9, 31:20/9, 41:20/9,\n 23:50/9, 32:100/9, 24:4/3, 34:212/9, 43:100/3.         (9)\n\nIts word is (1,1,1,1), and its full remainder is exactly\n\n (25/9)[||g_2-g_3||^2+4||g_3-g_4||^2]\n       +||U-J/4||^2+(55/144)||J||^2,\n U=g_0-g_1, J=g_2+g_3+g_4-3g_1.                        (10)\n\nThus the family does allow a nonzero new chord and a changed positive\nremainder. Equations(9)--(10) follow symbolically from (3)--(6); no model\nevaluation or arithmetic replay was run. Its constant-gradient kernel is\nthe only kernel, since the four displayed linear forms are independent\nmodulo a common gradient. This example is a calibration, not a competitive\nfour-step seed.\n\n## 4. Two-step signed tails cannot acquire extra capacity\n\nThis section rederives the capacity consequence of the older short-tail\nre-selection draft f3d96c901566e96b. It is NOT a new claim of priority and\ndoes not use that unverified draft as a premise.\n\nPut r=sqrt(2), lambda=1+r, and\n\n                 D2=Omega(1,lambda).\n\nSuppose a tail consists of u,v>=0 and has a real value-canceling full PSD\nidentity, with every coefficient nonnegative except possibly ell_02 and\nell_12. There is no lower bound on these two signed entries in this\nsection. Then\n\n                       1+u+v<=D2.                      (11)\n\nFirst let u,v>0, S=u+v and D=1+S. Let p=ell_01>=0 and q=ell_10>=0.\nThe two nonterminal kernel rows from (2) force\n\n ell_02=u(2-p)/S,\n ell_12=2(1+u)+q u/v>0.                                (12)\n\nThe first diagonal is\n\n M_00=u(2-u)+(1-u)(v p+2u)/S.                           (13)\n\nIf u>r, then u>1 and (13) is decreasing in p. At p=2 it is2-u^2<0.\nPSD therefore forces p<2, so (12) makes ell_02>0 also. The entire\nsupplied tail array is then nonnegative.\n\nIrrespective of the signs in (12), terminal outgoing coefficients are\nnonnegative. Its kernel moment and diagonal give\n\n S ell_20+v ell_21=2S(S+1),\n ell_20+ell_21>=S(S+2).\n\nSince S>=v, these imply v<=2D/(D+1). If u<=r, substitute\nv=D-(1+u) to obtain\n\n D^2-(2+u)D-(1+u)<=0,\n D<=Omega(1,1+u)<=Omega(1,lambda)=D2.\n\nIf u>r, the array has already been proved nonnegative. Put\nO=ell_01+ell_02. Its first moment gives 2u<=S O, and its first diagonal\ngives (u-1)O<=u(2-u). Therefore 2(u-1)<=S(2-u), equivalently\nu<=2D/(D+1). If v<=r, that bound\ngives D<=Omega(1,1+v)<=D2.\n\nIt remains to treat u>r and v>r. On f(x)=x^2/2 with initial point1,\nthe three gradients are1,1-u,(1-u)(1-v), and all finite Q vanish.\nThe identity sum h_i(2-h_i)g_i^2=1-g_2^2 gives\nV_b=1-D^2(1-u)^2(1-v)^2. Thus (1) and PSD give\n\n                 D (u-1)(v-1)<=1.                     (14)\n\nOn the other hand (u-r)(v-r)>0 implies\n\n (u-1)(v-1)>(r-1)(D-2-r).\n\nThe positive root D2 satisfies D2^2-(2+r)D2-(1+r)=0, hence\n(r-1)D2(D2-2-r)=1. The function (r-1)D(D-2-r) is strictly increasing\nfor D>=D2. Thus D>=D2 contradicts (14). This proves (11) for positive\nsteps, without assuming the tail is already primitive.\n\nFor u=0,v>0, the zero-step row forces ell_02=0. The terminal moment\ngives ell_20+ell_21=2(v+1), so its diagonal is2-v^2 and v<=r.\nFor v=0,u>0, the row at index1 forces ell_10=0, and the row at index0\nforces ell_01+ell_02=2. The first diagonal is2-u^2, so u<=r.\nFor u=v=0 the capacity is1. Thus all zero-step boundaries are included\nwithout identifying or deleting formal gradients.\n\n## 5. An exact obstruction for the ENTIRE parameterized family\n\nSet\n\n             C0=Omega(lambda,D2),\n             x0=C0-lambda-D2>0. It includes curved or distant variations; it is not a first-order\nnormal or a fixed-remainder assertion.\n\nBy (11), D<=D2. As x=C-A-D>=0, this is equivalent to\n\n C<=Omega(A,D)<=Omega(lambda,D2)=C0.\n\nNow suppose r<a<2 and C>=C0. Put eps=a^2-2>0 and K=(a-1)R/a>0,\nso mu=-eps+K Z. (17)\n\nThe exact simplification is\n\n HR=AD+Cx,\n sigma K^2=(a-1)^2[(AD)^2-(Cx)^2]/(a^2D^2),\n (a-1)^2 A^2-a^2(a^2-2)=1.\n\nConsequently (17) forces the especially small algebraic obstruction\n\n                        C(a-1)x<=D.                    (18)\n\nFor fixed a and x=C-A-D>=0, the expression\nC(a-1)(C-A-D)/D increases with C and decreases with D. The former follows\nfrom derivative (a-1)(2C-A-D)/D>0, and the latter follows by writing it\nas (a-1)[C(C-A)/D-C]. Therefore C>=C0,D<=D2 imply\n\n C(a-1)x/D >= (C0/D2)(a-1)(r+x0-a).\n\nThe source relation C0 x0=lambda D2 and lambda(r-1)=1 gives\n\n (C0/D2)(a-1)(r+x0-a)-1\n       =(C0/D2)(a-r)(1+x0-a).                          (19)\n\nWe have x0>1. Indeed D2>3, since its defining polynomial at3 is2-4r<0;\nthus (lambda-1)(D2-1)=r(D2-1)>2. The polynomial\nx(lambda+D2+x)-lambda D2 is strictly increasing for x>=0 and negative\nat1, proving x0>1. Since r<a<2<1+x0, (19) is strictly positive.\nThis contradicts (18). It excludes even equality C=C0 in the a>r case\nand completes the proof of (16).\n\nThe contradiction in (18)--(19) already ignores the additional upper\nbounds on Z in (5). Thus no adjustment of those nonnegative coefficients,\nno tail skew re-selection, and no increase of the tail PSD rank can repair\nthis family above C0.\n\nFor an explicit rational comparison, r<99/70 gives lambda<169/70.\nAt D=201/50, the polynomial D^2-(1+lambda)D-lambda is strictly greater\nthan362/17500>0, hence D2<201/50. At C=77/10,\n\n C^2-(169/70+201/50)C-(169/70)(201/50)=71/1750>0.\n\nMonotonicity of Omega yields\n\n                       C0<77/10<193/25.                 (20)\n\nThis proves the family cannot meet the required s>=168/25.\n\n## 6. Exact length-four ConPP calibration at the cap\n\nLet y=D2-1-lambda, so y>0 and y D2=lambda. Take the actual word\n\n                    h0=(r,1+x0,1+y,r).                 (21)\n\nIt has capacity C0. Put k2=(D2/lambda)^2, t2=k2-1,\nk4=(C0/D2)^2, and t4=k4-1. Thus (4) reproduces (22) in its original five\ncoordinates, rather than merely matching a scalar objective value.\n\nNo quadratic kernel was imposed during the construction or obstruction.\nAt this exact source the zero full matrix and its kernels are consequences\nof the displayed certificate. The rational example(9) has a different\nremainder and only the constant kernel.\n\n## 7. Restriction, counterexample to a global reading, and next interface\n\nThe declared family omits all tail-to-initial edges, all initial-to-tail\nedges except the terminal chord, and all bridge-to-tail edges except its\nterminal edge. It also forces the tail-to-bridge entries to be proportional\nto (b_0,...,b_(m-1),1). These are construction restrictions, NOT consequences\nof the full interface in04219554e41d9750.\n\nFor a concrete counterexample to the claim that every full certificate\nhas the proportionality restriction, consider the same unit word\n(1,1,1,1). Set, for i=0,1,2,3,\n\n lambda_(i,i+1)=2(i+1)^2,\n lambda_(i+1,i)=2(i+1)(i+2),\n\nwith all other entries0. Direct flow gives (-2,-2,-2,-2,8), and expansion\nof (1) gives sum_(i=0)^3 (i+1)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2. In its cut after\nindex1, the outgoing tail-to-bridge entries are (12,0,0), not proportional\nto (1,1,1). This array lies outside the family even though the same\nword also has the different inside-family array(9). Thus this example\nrefutes an array-level claim only; it does not prove that the restricted\nfamily fails to represent the existence of a certificate for some word.\n\nThe smallest obstruction to the attempted capacity gain is(18), together\nwith the short-tail cap and exact source factorization(19).
